# CLAIMS register

⚠ **`tapFailHard` in this document is THIS PROJECT'S SHORTHAND and appears zero times in rippled** — the real names are `fail_hard` (the RPC parameter) and `TapFailHard` (the internal apply flag). See [`docs/VOCABULARY.md`](docs/VOCABULARY.md). The code always used the real one.

**The register.** Every capability claim in a README, demo, post or conversation gets an entry here with the test result or primary citation that supports it. **Before publishing anything, diff the new claims against this register.**

**Human-gated checklist, not automation.** CI cannot judge whether prose overclaims. Do not add a CI job and call it solved.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Navigate** | [`docs/CLAIMS-INDEX.md`](docs/CLAIMS-INDEX.md) — one line per row, generated. ⚠ Do not hand-edit it; `make check-claims-index` fails if it drifts. |
| **Statuses** | `SUPPORTED` evidence attached · `PENDING` asserted, evidence not attached, **may not be published** · `BLOCKED` never claim · `RETRACTED` was claimed, found wrong — **keep the row** |
| **Sections** | [Standing prohibitions](#standing-prohibitions--never-claim) · [Established](#established) · [Pending](#pending--asserted-not-yet-supportable) · [Findings](#findings--verified-file-upstream-before-publishing) · [Retracted](#retracted) |
| **History** | [Revision history](#revision-history) — moved to the end 2026-09-04. ⚠ It was **5,358 characters of unbroken prose above the first heading**, so nothing in this file was visible without scrolling past it. |

---

## Standing prohibitions — never claim

| # | Claim | Why |
|---|---|---|
| P-1 | That this work makes XRPL quantum-safe or quantum-resistant | It does not |
| P-2 | That an application-layer PQ scheme protects funds at the ledger level | An adversary who breaks secp256k1 signs a valid classical transaction and the network accepts it |
| P-3 | That any amendment is live or enabled, without a dated `feature` RPC check | Amendment state is a reading, not knowledge |
| P-4 | That a Confidential MPT demo hides identities | It hides amounts and balances. Sender and receiver remain public |
| P-5 | That anything here is trustless, non-custodial, decentralised, audited, production-ready or secure | None of it is; ADR-0001 names the trusted parties |
| P-6 | That anything is compliant with regulation in a named jurisdiction | Not a compliance project |
| P-7 | That analysis of published work is original research | Cite Cenk, Malhotra, Akinyele, Chen, Xie, Qian by name where the work is theirs. **Add Tess Dore (2026-08-16):** the compact sigma protocol, the twisted-ElGamal analysis and the Bulletproofs++ analysis are Dore & Cenk, RippleX Research — Dore is *not* an XLS-0096 author and is easy to omit by copying E-1's list |
| P-8 | Any benchmark number without hardware, parameter set and methodology | Includes re-quoting XLS-0096 §14.2's "laptop-class CPU" timings — see B-1 |
| P-9 | That holders are private from the issuer | The issuer's decryption mirror is mandatory. No configuration makes this false |
| P-10 | That XLS-96 supports key rotation or key recovery | It supports neither, at any level |
| P-11 | That confidential transfers hide *who* transacts, or *when*, or *how often* | Only amounts are hidden. The counterparty graph and event timing are fully public |
| P-12 | **Any claim that a source does NOT say something — *nothing documents it*, *no such field*, *not exported*, *nobody has called it out*, *I could not find it* — without the search that would falsify it recorded alongside** | It is an assertion about a whole corpus and one search settles it. **Three graded rows failed this way on 2026-08-26** (E-155, E-156), two of them **positives**: U-15 quoted a sentence XLS-0096 has never contained; U-12 said a property was *"not called out anywhere I found"* when the spec states it **twice**, once in wording identical to the benefit claimed as an original reading. ⚠ **This shape survives review because it does not look like a claim** — a quotation has quotation marks, a benchmark visibly wants a methodology, and *"nobody has documented this"* reads as modesty. Gated by `tools/check-negative-claims.ts` |

## Established

| # | Claim | Evidence | Grade |
|---|---|---|---|
| E-1 | XLS-0096 authors: Cenk, Malhotra, Akinyele, Chen, Xie, Qian; status Draft, created 2026-01-15. ⚠ **Superseded upstream on 2026-09-03.** XLS-0096 PR #604, *"Update and finalize XLS-96"*, merged as `33ea4ca66` and set `status: Final`. It reads `Final` at `58ccd3047` and at `master` `47fa15357`, read on 2026-10-18 (E-322) | Spec header, at the time; the header at `9fbf7ef8e` (Draft) and at `33ea4ca66`, `58ccd3047` and `master` (Final) for the supersession | A |
| E-2 | Transaction types 85 Convert, 86 MergeInbox, 87 ConvertBack, 88 Send, 89 Clawback | `transactions.macro` @ 3.3.0, matches spec | A |
| E-3 | The Send `ZKProof` is a fixed-length bundle of a compact sigma proof plus an aggregated Bulletproof, enforced at preflight | `Protocol.h:466–471`; `ConfidentialMPTSend.cpp` length check | A |
| E-3a | Those components are 192 B and 754 B, totalling 946 B | **GAP-6 closed 2026-08-16.** `secp256k1_mpt.h:313` (192), `mpt_protocol.h:35` (754) at commit `48a3cfe` = `0.4.0-rc4`; identical at `1.0.4`. Independently **executed**: `test_bulletproof_agg` prints 754 B for m=2, 688 B for m=1. **Confirmed invariant in the participant count 2026-08-17: the `ZKProof` is 946 B at n=3 and 946 B at n=4**, as the structure implies — the sigma proof is six scalars regardless of how many recipients share the ciphertext randomness | **A** |
| E-4 | Total Send payload is **1210 B without an auditor and 1276 B with one — both now MEASURED.** The conflicting 1177 B is **wrong for the wire** | **RESOLVED 2026-08-17 by measurement.** Three `ConfidentialMPTSend` transactions serialised and accepted on standard Devnet. Each encrypted-amount field carries a **full 66-byte ElGamal pair**, not a bare 33-byte `C₂`: `SenderEncryptedAmount` 66, `DestinationEncryptedAmount` 66, `IssuerEncryptedAmount` 66, `AmountCommitment` 33, `BalanceCommitment` 33, `ZKProof` 946 = **1210 B**. The whole signed blob is **1423 B**. **Both documents were partly right and the reason matters (→ F-13):** `C₁` *is* shared, and this is **enforced by the protocol, not chosen by our harness** — a distinction REVIEW-004b HIGH-03 was right to demand and which the original evidence could not support, since it amounted to the harness observing its own input. Settled by negative control (REVIEW-004c §3): a Send built with independent per-participant randomness is refused at three layers — `mpt_get_confidential_send_proof` returns `-1` and generates nothing, `mpt_verify_send_proof` returns FAIL, and **rippled returns `tecBAD_PROOF`** where the shared-`C₁` control returns `tesSUCCESS`. The ledger nonetheless transmits `C₁` **once per participant**. The paper's 1177 is the size this would be if `C₁` were sent once, which rippled 3.3.0 does not do. The 99 B is exactly the 3 redundant 33-byte `C₁` copies at n=4. Our arithmetic was right; the paper's assumption about the wire was not. **The auditor case was measured on 2026-08-17**, closing the n=3 limitation the claims-check gate had flagged: an issuance carrying `sfAuditorEncryptionKey` was built and a four-participant Send accepted. Payload **exactly 1276 B** — `SenderEncryptedAmount` 66, `DestinationEncryptedAmount` 66, `IssuerEncryptedAmount` 66, **`AuditorEncryptedAmount` 66**, `AmountCommitment` 33, `BalanceCommitment` 33, `ZKProof` 946. Signed blob **1492 B** (n=3: 1423). The arithmetic prediction was confirmed rather than merely assumed, and **the paper's 1177 for n=4 is now refuted against a direct n=4 measurement rather than against a hypothetical** | **A on both figures, measured** |
| E-5 | **A confidential transaction costs exactly 10× the network's standard base fee.** The widely-quoted "100 drops" is that ratio against a `base_fee` of 10 and is **network-dependent, not absolute** | **Mechanism executed 2026-08-17 (REVIEW-004c §5), closing the "arithmetic not verified" gap that had stood since 2026-08-13.** Measured by ladder on Devnet where `fee` RPC reports `base_fee = 1`: `ConfidentialMPTSend` returns `telINSUF_FEE_P` at 1, 5 and 9 drops and `tesSUCCESS` at **10**, on both zero-amount and non-zero arms; a standard `AccountSet` from the same account is accepted at **1 drop**. Ratio 10:1, executed. `kConfidentialFeeMultiplier = 9` (`Protocol.h:492`); `Transactor::calculateBaseFee` still unread, but the result no longer depends on reading it. **Publish the ratio, never the absolute number** | **A on the ratio, executed; the "100 drops" figure is B and network-dependent** |
| E-6 | `kTxMaxSizeBytes = megabytes(1)` — size is not a binding constraint | `Protocol.h`, executed | A |
| E-7 | Confidential MPTs are incompatible with a non-zero `TransferFee` | Spec §6.4; enforced at `MPTokenIssuanceSet.cpp:214` | A |
| E-8 | Confidential MPTs cannot be used with the DEX, escrow or checks | Spec §A.4; RippleX docs | A |
| E-9 | Holder, issuer and auditor encryption keys cannot be rotated once registered | `tecDUPLICATE` / `tecNO_PERMISSION` paths; docs concur | A |

Read at `58ccd30` on 2026-10-18 (E-316, F-29) | Spec §7.4, at the time; §7.2 at `58ccd30` for the supersession | A |

| E-23 | ~~ElGamal decryption bounded at 1,000,000; above that permanently undecryptable through the API~~ **CORRECTED 2026-08-16 — see F-7.** Decryption brute-forces the discrete log over a **caller-supplied window** `[range_low, range_high]`. 1,000,000 is a *documented default*, not a bound. Cost is linear in window **width**, ~2.49 µs/iteration, with **no early exit** — paid in full on every call regardless of plaintext | Read + executed at `0.4.0-rc4` (`48a3cfe`): `src/elgamal.c:128,193–247,265–366`, `include/secp256k1_mpt.h:50–88`, `src/utility/mpt_utility.cpp:511–536`. Measured: `benchmarks-2026-08-16.md` §4 | **A** |
| E-22 | (a) A `ConfidentialMPTSend` of zero is **valid**. (b) It is indistinguishable from a non-zero send — **survived THREE falsification checks under a controlled rerun, NOT proven** | **Tested 2026-08-17, then RE-TESTED under control the same day after REVIEW-004 (see REVIEW-004c §2).** (a) is **Grade A, executed**: zero-amount Sends return `tesSUCCESS` and validate. (b) remains unprovable by this method; what can be said is that three attempts to break it failed. ⚠ **"Four checks" was wrong and is retired: the old check 1 was validity, which IS limb (a), so the count used the validity claim as evidence for the indistinguishability claim.** Both review legs missed this; the Gemini leg found it. Limb (b) rests on three checks, not four. **[1] Randomisation**, the sharpest test: two zero Sends from the same account at the same balance differ in **all six** observables — `SenderEncryptedAmount`, `DestinationEncryptedAmount`, `IssuerEncryptedAmount`, `AmountCommitment`, `BalanceCommitment`, `ZKProof`. Not canonical. **[2] F-1 distinguisher** — canonical `EncZero` recomputed independently for both sender and destination matches **no** zero-Send ciphertext. **[3] Side-by-side vs non-zero Sends, CONTROLLED.** The first run was confounded — both legs caught it — comparing a 100-unit send proved at balance 250 against zero sends proved at 150, with n=1 on the control and the fee dimension argued rather than measured. Re-run with zero sends **first**, since a zero send does not move the balance: **all four arms proved at balance 80**, n=2 per arm. Result: signed blob **1423 B all four**, ZKProof **946 B all four**, `AffectedNodes` **3 all four**, identical fields mutated on identical node types (`ConfidentialBalanceInbox`+`IssuerEncryptedBalance` on the destination; `ConfidentialBalanceSpending`+`ConfidentialBalanceVersion`+`IssuerEncryptedBalance` on the sender), version **+1 all four**. **Fee threshold measured on both arms**, not deduced: a full ladder gives `telINSUF_FEE_P` at 1 and 9 drops and `tesSUCCESS` at 10 for **both** zero and non-zero — identical. **Metadata is NOT constant and E-51 was wrong about it**, but the variation tracks *destination state*, not amount: at matched destination state the arms are byte-identical (1219/1219, then 1225/1225 once the destination has merged). **No asymmetry attributable to the amount was found.** Zero sends still mutate the sender's spending ciphertext, because subtracting `Enc(0, r)` changes `C₁` — so "nothing changed" is not observable either. **Never write "confirmed": four passing checks are not a proof of indistinguishability, and an observer with resources this session did not model may still separate them** | **(a) A, executed. (b) C — survived three checks under a controlled rerun, unfalsified, not proven** |

Full Send bundle ≈ 34.8 ms prove / 16.4 ms verify | Measured 2026-08-16 — `docs/task-zero/benchmarks-2026-08-16.md`, full hardware + flags + spread recorded per P-8 | A |

The script names a TS wrapper `@xrplf/mpt-crypto` and states the work is driven by xrpl.js needing to batch XLS-0096 transactions (PR #138, 2026-08-14) | A |
| E-32 | ~~The `@xrplf/mpt-crypto` npm package referenced by that build script is **not published** as at 2026-08-16~~ | **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-17 → E-53. `@xrplf/mpt-crypto@0.1.0` was published 2026-08-16T15:26:21Z** — hours after the query that produced this row, which is why it read `Not found`. True when made, false within the day. **This is the third time in three sessions** that an ecosystem-gap claim expired inside 24 hours (see U-8, U-9). The register's own rule — re-check registries immediately before filing, never at research time — is now evidenced three times and should be treated as binding | **Superseded** |
| E-33 | **Generating a `ConfidentialMPTSend` proof requires the sender's ElGamal secret key `sk_A` as a witness.** `sk_A` appears in the witness `w = (m, r, b, ρ, sk_A)`; balance linkage is proved as `B₂ − b·G = sk_A·B₁` precisely because the historical randomness `r_b` is unavailable to the prover. 2-special soundness extracts `sk_A`; knowledge soundness holds in the ROM under DL. **ADR-0001's central premise is confirmed.** `ConvertBack` shares the relation; `Clawback` requires `sk_iss` instead | `cmpt-compact-sigma.pdf` §2.3, §2.4 Eq. 1, Thm 4.2 (Relations 5–6), Thm 4.4, Remark 4.5 — Dore & Cenk, RippleX Research, 2026-05-12. Read in full 2026-08-16 | A |
| E-34 | **Nonce reuse in a compact-form sigma proof leaks `sk_A`, and is undetectable from the proofs.** Compact form transmits `(e, z…)` not the commitments, so reused nonces yield proofs that verify normally while allowing an observer holding both to extract the witness. ⚠ **That mitigation line is FALSE and was falsified by two independent cryptography reviewers on 2026-08-17 (REVIEW-005a, REVIEW-005b, adjudicated in REVIEW-005c §1).** `statement` does not include `TransactionContextID` as the paper defines it, while the Fiat–Shamir challenge does — so an ordinary resubmission at a changed sequence or `CB_S_Version`, with cached ciphertexts, yields two proofs sharing first-round commitments under distinct challenges. That is Theorem 4.2's extraction condition exactly. **The formula as written causes the failure it is quoted to prevent.** The correct requirement is that the nonce preimage must refine the challenge preimage — every byte that can influence the challenge, other than the commitments, must appear injectively in the derivation. See F-17 | `cmpt-compact-sigma.pdf` §6.1, Remark 6.1. **Direct input to ADR-0001 §(e) and the Rust signer — ranks above TOB-RIPCTX-5, since it needs only a coding error, not an adversary with local measurement** | A |
| E-35 | Compact-form proofs **cannot be batch-verified** — commitments are reconstructed per-proof from each proof's own challenge, leaving no cross-proof linear structure. Verification is O(n) multi-scalar multiplications per proof (11 at n=4), with no amortisation across a ledger | `cmpt-compact-sigma.pdf` Remark 6.2. Constrains any throughput claim | A |
| E-36 | XLS-96 **deliberately rejected twisted ElGamal.** Under compact form its size advantage shrinks from 292 B to an irreducible **64 B** — structural, arising from three scalar witnesses vs five — judged insufficient against specification, implementation and audit costs | `cmpt-compact-sigma.pdf` §A, Tables 5–6, §A.5. The scheme choice was quantified, not incidental — relevant background for W-PQ | A |

*E-45 to E-52 added 2026-08-17 by the first session to generate proofs and submit Confidential MPT transactions. Eight confidential transactions on standard Devnet; every row below is re-queryable by `tx` / `account_objects`.*

| # | Claim | Evidence | Grade |

The utility wrapper **rejects a NULL context hash** before forwarding, and `build_pok_challenge` folds it into the Fiat–Shamir challenge `SHA-256("CMPT_POK_SK_REGISTER" ‖ pk(33) ‖ T(33) ‖ context_id(32))`. The context hash itself is `sha512_half(u16(txtype) ‖ acc(20) ‖ iss(24) ‖ u32(seq) ‖ acc(20) ‖ u32(0))` — the account appears **twice**. The proof therefore binds **(account, issuance, sequence)**: not copyable across accounts, not replayable across issuances, not replayable at a different sequence | **Read at source 2026-08-18**, rippled tag `3.3.0` (commit `00a178f`) and mpt-crypto `85dbac7` = `1.0.4`: `ConfidentialMPTConvert.cpp:74,153–162`; `ConfidentialTransfer.cpp:366–375`; `mpt_utility.cpp:286–297,349–369,859–883`; `proof_pok_sk.c` `build_pok_challenge`. The chain is five links of source and the NULL guard is explicit, but the executed form would be stronger | **A, read** |

(2) A leg ruled that `b58_encode`'s trailing cleanup strips legitimate payload digits equal to the zero character, corrupting addresses. The `% 32` wrap in `OneShotAux` was therefore guarded by nothing. **Fixed by moving the check out of the test and into the code**: `fill_bytes` now **panics** rather than recycling, with unit tests either side of the 32-byte boundary, because continuing with weakened nonce entropy is worse than stopping | **Self-caught 2026-08-20**, before any review round saw it — by asking what the test would do if the property were false, which is the question `E-108` was supposed to have taught. ⚠ **Nobody had reviewed this code yet**; it was written *after* `REVIEW-018`'s rulings, so the rotation that ruled the design never saw the implementation | A, executed |
| E-114 | ⚠ **ADR-0002's open question may be premised on a facility TASK ZERO §9 does not describe, and two review rounds could not see it because the packet omitted §9.** The ADR asks *"is investor-side confidentiality in scope?"* and its table contrasts *"individual investor's contribution"* — **not on ledger, one funder** — against the hybrid's public `VaultDeposit` each. §9 read at source says: *"The funding bank issues the MPT and creates a Dedicated Account… The bank funds the vault publicly with **the whole facility float**"*, and mentions investors only as parties who need to *see* total facility size. **One funder.** If that is the design, the hybrid's entire benefit — protocol-native share accounting, pool accounting, withdrawal policy across many investors — solves a problem this facility does not have, while its costs (a second protocol family, the pseudo-account domain exemption, a pseudo-account that cannot convert) are real. **The scope question does not disappear; it relocates**, to the much easier *does this facility have one funder or many?* | **Read at source 2026-08-20**, `TASK-ZERO-2026-08-13.md` §9 steps 1–2. ⚠ **A packet defect, not a leg defect** — `REVIEW-017`'s packet carried the ADR and not the section it summarises, so neither leg could check the premise. Same class as the E-101 omission `REVIEW-016`'s Codex leg flagged. **Still not a coding session's call**, but it is a far cheaper question to answer | A, source |
| E-115 | **ADR-0002's hybrid is resolved on a TECHNICAL ground, and the scope question two revisions deferred to turns out not to decide it.** Rev 2 and rev 3 both framed the XLS-65 `Vault` alternative as turning on *"is investor-side confidentiality in scope?"*. It does not: **blocker 1 makes the hybrid a hard fork rather than a tunable tradeoff** — `VaultCreate` builds the share issuance's flags itself and never sets `lsfMPTCanHoldConfidentialBalance`, and a pseudo-account cannot sign `MPTokenIssuanceSet` to add it — so it forces **native share accounting XOR confidential investor positions**. Three things then settle it with no scope call: the accounting is commodity and the confidentiality is the differentiator; §9 already forces `TransferFee = 0` and settles interest off-ledger, so share-price arithmetic was never going on-chain; and **accountability does not require publishing positions** — the public aggregate (`sfConfidentialOutstandingAmount`), the mandatory issuer mirror and the optional auditor key already provide three tiers, and the hybrid adds disclosure without adding accountability. ⚠ **Therefore the funder count stops mattering**: one funder or twenty, all are confidential MPT holders and the architecture does not fork (E-114) | **Ruled 2026-08-20** by Nandan on this reasoning; rev 4 merges Decisions A and B. ⚠ **Third position this ADR has taken on the hybrid** — rev 1 rejected it on an argument that was wrong, rev 2–3 called it an open scope question, rev 4 rejects it on blocker 1. ⚠ **Reversal condition, narrower and more checkable than the scope question it replaces:** if `lsfMPTCanHoldConfidentialBalance` ever becomes settable on a `Vault` share issuance, the discharge is void. **ADR-0002 remains `Proposed`** on item 4 alone | A, executed |

| E-117 | ⚠ **Rejecting the XLS-65 `Vault` traded away a custody property, and ~~ADR-0002 does not say so~~ ADR-0002 has said so since `c34dcec` (E-256, E-295).** A `Vault`'s pseudo-account holds pooled assets **with no key** — nothing can move them outside the protocol's rules. The Dedicated Account that replaces it is an **ordinary XRPL account with an ordinary key**, so whoever holds that key can move the entire float. Rev 4 rejected the hybrid on blocker 1 (share tokens cannot be confidential) and that reasoning stands. ⚠ ~~*the comparison table counts only disclosure and accounting; it has no row for custody*~~ **— STRUCK 2026-09-09, and it was false from the commit that wrote this row.** `c34dcec` added **both** E-117 and the `Custody of the pooled float` row it says is missing, so the clause described a state its own commit had already ended and then read as live for twenty days. So it is not merely that nobody holds a key: the master is disabled, deposit authorisation is on, and the sequence number cannot support a transaction. The second premise is `facility/src/facility.ts:129`, which runs `checkSeedControlsAddress` over `bank` and `dedicated` alike. ~~**What is live is the last clause and only it: the facility's float sits under one key and no review has asked who holds it**~~ **Discharged 2026-09-13 (E-294): `REVIEW-046` asked who holds it and found more holders than one key, and the owner ruled B-27, accepting the risk by name with a printed disclosure.** What was live was the last clause and only it — P-12 search, 2026-09-09, over ~~357~~ 204 files in `docs/reviews/` (E-292): the only `custod` matches inside `REVIEW-019`, `REVIEW-022` and `REVIEW-023` are boilerplate quotations of P-5 in ~~two REQUEST documents~~ three REQUEST documents and one leg (E-292), and no leg or adjudication in any of the three rounds weighs it. ⚠ **A decision request now exists** ~~and this clause is still live~~ (discharged 2026-09-13, below): `docs/decisions/DECISION-REQUEST-e117-custody-of-the-float-2026-09-11.md` (2026-09-11) states the question and prices the options, and it establishes that the seed confers a **second** power this row never names — permanent, unrotatable decryption of every per-supplier amount (E-266). A request is not a review, so nothing here is discharged. The request has since had a review of its own, `REVIEW-046`, which withdrew its recommendation and found the question wider than this row states (E-290), and that does not discharge this clause either | A, source on both premises; ~~the custody axis itself remains unreviewed~~ the custody axis was reviewed by `REVIEW-046` and `REVIEW-047` and ruled as B-27 (E-294, E-295) |

**Run 1 — killed.** `OOMKilled=true`, exit 137, 1h31m, ~3.9 GiB RSS with `node_size small` in a **7.75 GiB** Docker VM (Docker Desktop's default half of an 18 GiB host). `complete_ledgers: empty`.

**Run 2 — the VM raised to 11.67 GiB, same node, resuming from its own 6.3 GB node store.** ⚠ **It survived and it still did not converge.** One hour: memory plateaued at **6.3 GiB** (no kill), the node store grew **6.3 → 6.9 GB**, `complete_ledgers` stayed **`empty` for every one of 110 samples**, and by the end the RPC was **timing out at 8 s** while CPU sat at **1663% of 1200%** — pegged, with rippled dumping its own job-queue report. **Memory was a ceiling and not the constraint.**

⚠ **So the honest conclusion is not "needs more RAM" and this row said that for one commit.** Under x86 emulation the node cannot acquire the state tree faster than Devnet mutates it, and neither of the two levers a config file has closed the gap. **What is left is a native arm64 rippled build, different hardware, or a hosted node the operator controls — none of which is a coding-session decision.** ⚠ **Swap is not a lever either**: a node already saturating twelve cores will not be rescued by paging. ⚠ **A standalone rippled is not a fallback**, and the reason is a property of this project: `signer/src/ledger.rs` hardcodes `DEVNET_NETWORK_ID = 2` and *"refuses every network but Devnet, at any phase, for any reason"*, so a local network would have to **claim to be Devnet** to drive the signer — the substitution this project forbids. Separately, `[features] ConfidentialTransfer` in a standalone config came back `enabled: false, vetoed: true` (→ U-49) | **2026-08-25.** `tools/loopback-node/` is the node, committed with its config and the reason for every line. **Falling back to the public endpoint was available and was refused *by this session*** — ⚠ **and then ruled, taken and disclosed**, because the project owner amended B-15's answer the same day (**E-151**). Round two found this sentence stating the project's final position and getting it backwards; what is true is that a coding session may not substitute a source on its own authority. The whole content of B-15's answer is that the public endpoint is not sufficient for the facility. ⚠ **The honest output is a named gap, not a substituted source** | A, executed — the *cost* is what was executed |

| E-149 | **`fail_hard` on the `submit` RPC is what sets `TapFailHard`, and a `tec` under it is discarded and never relayed. | E-150 | ⚠ **The facility was reviewed in two rounds and the loopback obligation it exists to keep was ESCAPABLE TWO WAYS — neither of them a defect in the check.** `REVIEW-022`, three legs. (a) **CRITICAL**: `signer/src/main.rs:71` gives `WCONF_LEDGER_ENDPOINT` **precedence over the profile**, and the facility inherited `process.env` while setting only the profile — so the facility's own reads could be loopback while **the signer**, the component that reads the destination's and the issuer's keys, read a remote node, with `assertLoopbackProfile` checking a value nothing used and `quietSigner` swallowing the mandatory disclosure. Neither defect is in the class the step-8 gate checks: it asserts what the tier **cannot reach**, and these were about what it **actually talks to**. **A gate over a named property is not a substitute for running the artifact** | A, measured |
| E-210 | **The issuer view DECRYPTS, and the reason it did not was a comment that was wrong about its own repository.** `web/api/issuer.ts` said decryption was *"delegated to the CLI rather than reimplemented — a second implementation of the dlog search is a second thing to drift (E-141)"*. ⚠ **There was no second implementation to avoid**: `facility/src/mptool.ts` already **exports** `decryptInRange`, which shells out to the one real `mptool` binary. **Importing it is composition — the same pattern the same session used one file over**, where `ledger-view.ts` composes `loopback.ts` rather than reimplementing B-15's obligation. ⚠⚠ **AND THE REAL BLOCKER WAS SOMETHING ELSE ENTIRELY, FOUND ONLY BY TRYING IT: THE ISSUER KEY DOES NOT DECRYPT THE HOLDER'S BALANCE CIPHERTEXT.** `mptool decrypt` with the issuer's ElGamal secret against `ConfidentialBalanceSpending` returns **`rc=-1`**; against `IssuerEncryptedBalance` it returns the balance. **They are different ciphertexts of the same value under different keys** — and `facility/src/loopback.ts` was not reading the mirror at all, because the facility reconciles each party's OWN balance with that party's own key, which is a different question from what the issuer can see | **Fixed and EXECUTED against live Devnet 2026-08-28**, issuance `004625C9…332CB25E8`: all four demo accounts decrypt — **75, 0, 270, 778** — from `IssuerEncryptedBalance`, beside public plaintext `MPTAmount` of 750, 586, 30 and 0. ⚠ **Cross-checked rather than assumed**: holder A's mirror gives **75** and holder A's OWN ElGamal key against its OWN `ConfidentialBalanceSpending` also gives **75**, which is what establishes that the mirror tracks the spending balance. **P-9 is now demonstrated rather than asserted** — the same holder the observer page shows as an opaque ciphertext | A, measured |
| E-211 | ⚠⚠ **A FALSE DISCLOSURE SENTENCE SHIPPED ON THE OBSERVER PAGE, AND IT IS F-24's SHAPE A THIRD TIME.** For any ciphertext not matching the canonical `EncZero`, the page said *"**Non-zero**, and the amount is hidden from an observer."* **That is false.** Measured on Devnet 2026-08-28: holder `rDYMkRMh7o7QfkNaoGvtd7TgFzmt12FEM8` decrypts to **0** through the issuer mirror, while its `ConfidentialBalanceSpending` does **not** match the canonical `EncZero` recomputed from its own registered key. ⚠⚠ **A BALANCE CAN BE EXACTLY ZERO AND NOT PUBLICLY IDENTIFIABLE AS ZERO.** F-1's canonical form is used on **first-time init, merge reset and clawback**; a balance that reached zero **by spending** is a homomorphic result — a randomised encryption of zero that matches nothing and looks identical to any other hidden value. ⚠⚠ **`tools/observer-harness.ts:277` HAD IT RIGHT AND THE WEB TIER FLATTENED THE HEDGE**: *"not the canonical zero (some non-zero value, or never reset)"* became *"Non-zero"*. **The correct source was one import away, in a file this module already imports `canonicalEncZero` from.** ⚠ **The wording tests did not catch it because it is a SEMANTIC error, not a phrasing one** — every guard was about privacy claims, and this is a claim about arithmetic. ⚠⚠ **AND THE PROTOCOL FACT WAS ALREADY IN THIS PROJECT'S OWN UPSTREAM REGISTER**: `UPSTREAM-FEEDBACK.md` **U-47** — *"a confidential balance that has been emptied by `ConvertBack` is not the canonical `EncZero`"* — observed on Devnet and written up before this tier existed. **So this is not a fact the project had to discover; it is one it had recorded, filed under its own heading, and then contradicted on a screen.** That is E-141's shape at the level of a whole register rather than a count | **Corrected 2026-08-28.** The note now states that a non-match means the value cannot be determined, that this is **not** the same as non-zero, and that a spent-to-zero balance looks exactly the same; | E-212 | ⚠⚠ **THE WEB TIER SHIPPED A DECRYPTION ORACLE, AND IT VIOLATED THE SCOPE THE SAME SESSION HAD WRITTEN HOURS EARLIER.** `web/api/issuer.ts` read `WCONF_ELGAMAL_SK` from the environment and decrypted **per request**, behind an unauthenticated `GET /api/issuer/:account/:issuanceId` — **the caller chooses the account and receives a decrypted confidential balance.** ⚠ **Architecture §6 step 8 says the tier may touch *"the issuer-view decryption tool's OUTPUT"* and may never touch *"any private key"*, and `CLAUDE.md` says the issuer-view tool is *"a single CLI command … Not a standalone application"*.** ⚠⚠ **AND `ADR-0003` NAMES THIS EXACTLY, AS ITS FOURTH ROTATION TRIGGER**: *"a tier that accepts an issuer key over HTTP has built a decryption oracle"* — written by the same session, one file over. **The trigger was written about the key ARRIVING over HTTP; the oracle is the RESULT LEAVING over HTTP, and the author read their own warning as narrower than it was.** ⚠ `/meta`'s `holdsKeys: false` was false while it ran, and **CORS is not a defence** — it constrains browsers, and `curl` is not a browser. ⚠ **A second exposure rode along**: the chosen helper `decryptInRange` spawns `mptool decrypt <ciphertext> <sk> …`, and **argv is world-readable via `ps`** — the precise exposure `tools/issuer-decrypt-balance.ts` refuses a `--key` flag to avoid, reintroduced by the helper picked to avoid a *different* problem. ⚠⚠ **`@xrplf/mpt-crypto`'s `decryptAmount` is IN-PROCESS and is what that CLI already uses**, so the better path was in the file the rule came from | **Found by Codex review 2026-08-28, three P1s, and the diagnosis was correct on all three.** **Fixed by removing the capability, not guarding it:** `tools/issuer-view-snapshot.ts` holds the key, decrypts the **curated demo set** in-process via `decryptAmount`, and writes **results**; ≤300 words of prose with one concrete ask; no positives-only filings to a bug tracker; no negative claim without its recorded search; short sentences and no project shorthand outward. `tools/check-filing.ts` enforces what is mechanical. ⚠ **`docs/upstream-filings/TEMPLATE.md` is the POSITIVE CONTROL and must PASS** — a gate that only ever fails is as useless as one that only ever passes, and **the template caught two real defects**: `prose()` counted **HTML comments** as the opening paragraph, and the early-ask check looked for a **REQUEST** when the protocol wants the **DEFECT** stated first, so a well-formed draft opening *"the page says X, the spec says the opposite"* was failed for not saying *"please"*. ⚠ **A gate that fails its own template is measuring the wrong thing.** Two more: the sentence splitter read a `###` heading plus a bullet list as one **110-word sentence**, and the runner was **unguarded**, so importing the detectors ran the whole gate — the exact flaw that forced `check-zeroize-bounds.py`'s stripper to be re-derived rather than shared (E-207). ⚠⚠ **AND THE BREAK-TEST HARNESS LIED TWICE**: two mutations reported *"NOT CAUGHT"* when one anchor **did not exist in the file** and the other placed a bare negative **inside the paragraph that already carried the `grep`**, which the detector correctly excuses. **The harness now asserts the anchor applied and reports VOID rather than passing** | **2026-09-01.** Template PASSES, the 2026-08-26 dev-portal filing FAILS on three of five rules (503 words, an unsearched negative, two run-on sentences) — which is the outcome that makes the protocol worth having. **Five break-tests, all CAUGHT**, each with the template proved green before and after. ⚠ **`whatThisDoesNotSay()` prints in the gate's output**: it cannot tell whether a claim is TRUE, and every defect in the 2026-08-26 round was a false or overstated claim that counting words would never have caught. No May revision under any name; a web search returned only secondary coverage of the April report. **The gap is now bounded rather than unknown.** Upstream cites two of its findings in source: **TOB-RIPCTXR-1** (`secp256k1_mpt.h:42–43`) — TOB-RIPCTX-1/-4 decryption timing side channels *reintroduced*, mitigated by fixed-iteration search; and **TOB-RIPCTXR-10** (`mpt_internal.h:188,199–201`) — Informational, the unprefixed nonce-KDF IKM layout as a brittle-API concern, *"not a present-day exploit"*. **Neither is keygen and neither is randomness sourcing**, so on present evidence this document does not gate ADR-0001 §(e) — a statement about the two findings we can see, not about the report's full contents. **Never cite RIPCTXR identifiers as our own reading**; attribute them to upstream's comment. Same discipline as B-6. ⚠ The heading's *"two findings upstream cites by identifier"* counts source comments only. Upstream's tracker cites seven more of the revision's findings, and three of its appendices (E-306) |
| B-1 | ~~Bulletproof proving ~44.8 ms, verification ~22.6 ms~~ | **RESOLVED 2026-08-16 → E-24.** The spec's §14.2 figures remain unusable under P-8 and are still never to be quoted. Replaced by measured numbers with full hardware and parameters |
| B-2 | ~~"ConfidentialTransfer is not enabled on Mainnet"~~ | **RESOLVED 2026-08-16 → E-25.** GAP-1 closed |
| B-3 | ~~"The Confidential Devnet has ConfidentialTransfer, PermissionedDomains and SingleAssetVault enabled"~~ | **RESOLVED 2026-08-16 → E-26**, with the premise corrected: there is no dedicated Confidential Devnet. All three are enabled on **standard Devnet** |
| B-4 | "MPTokensV1 activated in October 2025" | Secondary coverage only |
| B-5 | "rippled 3.3.0 released ~2026-08-07, voting under way" | Secondary coverage only. Source confirms the code, not the date or the votes |
| B-6 | Anything about computational vs unconditional hiding in XLS-96 | ePrint 2026/602 unread (GAP-2) |
| B-7 | ~~Any statement about what the 192-byte sigma proof proves — including ADR-0001's premise that balance linkage requires the sender's secret key~~ | **RESOLVED 2026-08-16 → E-33/E-34. GAP-3 closed**, paper read in full |
| B-9 | ~~Confidential transaction metadata stays within `kOversizeMetaDataCap = 5200`~~ | **RESOLVED 2026-08-17 → E-51. Measured, not estimated:** a three-participant `ConfidentialMPTSend` writes **1219 B** of metadata, 23% of the cap. Not a binding constraint at n=3 |
| B-10 | Scheduled cover traffic conceals drawdown frequency | **Not blocked — E-22 was tested on 2026-08-17 and survived (§ E-22).** B-10 stays `PENDING`, and its dependency is now precisely stated rather than merely inherited: cover traffic needs E-22(b), *indistinguishability*, which **cannot be proven by this method** — three falsification attempts failed, which is not the same thing. E-22(a), *validity*, is settled Grade A and is the part that would have killed B-10 outright. **What B-10 still needs is an adversary model nobody has built**: the three checks compare one transaction against another in isolation, even after the controlled rerun. They say nothing about *traffic analysis over a sequence* — timing, counterparty graph, `DestinationTag`, or correlation between a holder's sends and the issuer's plaintext `ConfidentialOutstandingAmount`. Do not upgrade B-10 on the strength of E-22 alone. **TASK ZERO §9, which carries the recommendation itself, was corrected on 2026-08-17 to match this row — it had continued to assert indistinguishability as settled, and that mismatch between register and prose was the highest-severity finding of REVIEW-004** |
| B-11 | **XLS-96 and XLS-66 provide no encrypted loan accounting as at rippled 3.3.0**, and adding it would require a new proof relation, a new specification and validator-side changes | **Rewritten 2026-08-17 after REVIEW-003 — the previous wording claimed this was *structurally impossible* and that claim is withdrawn, see R-6.** What remains asserted is scope and cost, not impossibility. Still `PENDING`: the two checks named on 2026-08-16 are undone — that no multiplicative or comparison operation is exported by `secp256k1_mpt.h`, and whether XLS-0096's authors have said anything about arithmetic beyond addition. **Do not restore the word "structural" without new evidence** |

*(B-8 retired: GAP-4 resolved. The mechanism is E-17/E-19; the remaining question is which amendments are enabled, now B-3.)*

---

## Findings — verified, file upstream before publishing

Documentation errors, specification errata, and — new on 2026-08-16 — three findings against the **mpt-crypto library and its build**, from the first session with a built tree. F-1..F-6 were each checked against the rippled 3.3.0 transactors; the implementation agrees with the specification every time. F-7..F-9 are read and executed at mpt-crypto `0.4.0-rc4` (`48a3cfe`).

**F-3 and F-4 are retired — see R-3 and R-4.** They are kept as rows because the register keeps retracted claims.

| # | Finding | Evidence | Grade |
|---|---|---|---|
| F-1 | Docs claim the reset inbox value is indistinguishable; it is the canonical `EncZero`, recomputable by any observer from public inputs, and used on first-time init, merge reset and clawback | `ConfidentialMPTConvert.cpp:305–313`, `MergeInbox.cpp:111–114`, `Clawback.cpp:138–166`. **Executed on Devnet 2026-08-17 → E-49**: recomputed independently and byte-matched against a live `sfConfidentialBalanceSpending`. No longer a source reading | A |
| F-2 | Spec §13.2.2's "separate view keys" have no ledger field. Protocol layer: issuer keys are permanent. Operational layer: an issuer choosing Mechanism 2 grants disclosure that can never be withdrawn | `ledger_entries.macro`; RippleX docs | A / B |
| F-3 | Docs say confidentiality can be toggled on and off; spec and transactor have no clear flag | `MPTokenIssuanceSet.cpp` flag handling | A |
| F-4 | Docs say clawback resets the version counter to 0; it increments | `ConfidentialMPTClawback.cpp:150` | A |
| F-5 | Spec §11.1 names a `RevealedAmount` field that does not exist; the field is `sfMPTAmount` | `sfields.macro`, executed | A |
| F-6 | Spec §11.2 says clawed-back funds go to the issuer's reserve; §11/§11.4 and the transactor say burned | `ConfidentialMPTClawback.cpp:170–179` | A |
| F-7 | **The 1,000,000 decryption "bound" is not a bound.** It is a caller-supplied window `[range_low, range_high]`; the audit's "the caller still passes a magic 1000000" **no longer holds at `0.4.0-rc4`** — no caller in the tree passes any literal, and the only `1000000` outside test data is a doc comment recommending it as a default. A 10¹⁶ balance decrypts in **5.09 ms** given a 2 001-wide window. **No value is permanently unrecoverable.** What is expensive is *uncertainty*, not magnitude: resynchronising a lost balance costs ~2.49 µs per unit of range, with no early exit | Read + executed at `48a3cfe`: `src/elgamal.c:128,193–247,265–366`, `src/utility/mpt_utility.cpp:511–536`; measured in `benchmarks-2026-08-16.md` §4. Corrects E-23 and `ADDENDUM-2026-08-16.md` §1. Still holds at mpt-crypto `1.0.5` on reading (E-299). Ruled not-to-file (E-302) | A |
| F-8 | **mpt-crypto documents an escape hatch that does not exist.** `secp256k1_mpt.h:73` directs callers needing larger ranges to `secp256k1_elgamal_decrypt_bsgs`, and `elgamal.c:279` repeats the advice. **No such symbol exists at `0.4.0-rc4` or `1.0.4`** — the string appears nowhere in either tree but those two comments. The library ships no sublinear DLP path while its own documentation implies one | `grep -rn bsgs` over both tags returns only the two comments; `secp256k1_elgamal_decrypt` is the sole exported decrypt symbol. Fixed in upstream's source tree from tag `1.0.5`, where the function exists, and whether the header reaches consumers is unchecked (U-56); still true at this project's `1.0.4` pin (E-297). Recorded not-to-file (E-298) | A |
| F-9 | **mpt-crypto's committed `conan.lock` ~~cannot be resolved from public remotes~~ does not resolve from conancenter, or from the remote rippled's setup adds (E-300).** It pins `openssl/3.6.2#4789bbf1…`; conancenter carries 3.6.2 only at revision `36f92686…`. `secp256k1/0.7.1` is not on conancenter at all. Neither the Ripple remote nor this constraint is in the README's Prerequisites | Executed 2026-08-16; `benchmarks-2026-08-16.md` §5. Re-run 2026-09-14 at `1.0.5`: the `secp256k1` half is answered by the setup the README links, and the `openssl` half still fails with that setup (E-300). Re-run 2026-09-22 with the same result. The remedy is the remote migration `clio#3118` made, which mpt-crypto has not, and the old remote is still live, so it is a migration and not a retirement (E-313). Ruled to file (E-302). **Filed 2026-09-28 as [mpt-crypto#148](https://github.com/XRPLF/mpt-crypto/issues/148)** after two review rounds (E-314) | A |
| F-10 | **`LendingProtocol` and `ConfidentialTransfer` are silently non-composable.** Both are enabled on the same network.
