No error, no warning, no mention in XLS-0065, XLS-0066 or XLS-0096. The reverse direction **is** diagnosed — `ConfidentialMPTConvert.cpp:83–87` returns `tecNO_PERMISSION` when confidentiality is not enabled | `docs/task-zero/XLS-66-INVESTIGATION-2026-08-16.md` §4; 19 Devnet transactions 2026-08-16, plus **the full fifteen-transactor lifecycle on 2026-08-17 (E-40)**. The evidence base is now execution, not a grep. **Reachability tested directly, after both REVIEW-007 legs objected — correctly — that fifteen successes prove NON-REJECTION, not unreachability.** In the original lifecycle both lending actors were funded *publicly*, so no actor ever held confidential value and reachability was never exercised. Closed by experiment: a **borrower holding 5 public and 600 confidential units of the same asset** was refused a 120-unit `LoanPay` with **`tecINSUFFICIENT_FUNDS`**, and its confidential balance came back **byte-identical** with the version counter unmoved. Together with the same result on `VaultDeposit` (E-50), reachability is now directly tested on **two transactors — one vault, one lending** — and refused on both. Evidence hashes persisted; `LoanPay` refusal `475D8E6B31F9EAD1595B0762A738EB4A1CB7E8DF9EF14FD7AA976E5FB1C1EE9D` | **A on the executed scope.** ⚠ **Scope stated precisely, because both legs caught the earlier inflation: 15/15 for acceptance and non-mutation; 2/15 for direct reachability testing.** The other thirteen remain inference from those two plus the source reading. ⚠ **Still do not file.** The coverage objection is answered; **the competing reading is not, and it is now the sole blocker:** that an issuance may legitimately be confidential-capable while holding a public balance, so a public vault operating on that public balance is intended composability rather than a defect. If that reading is right, this is a documentation request, not a finding. Settle it before filing |
| F-11 | `ConfidentialMPTClawback` has **no** pseudo-account guard, where ordinary `Clawback` returns `tecPSEUDO_ACCOUNT`. Unreachable by construction rather than rejected — a pseudo-account can never acquire `sfHolderEncryptionKey` (E-39). Recorded because "unreachable by construction" and "explicitly rejected" are different assurances, and only the second survives a refactor | `Clawback.cpp:196–199` vs `ConfidentialMPTClawback.cpp:55–98`. Still holds at rippled `develop` on reading (E-303). Ruled not-to-file (E-308) | A |
| F-17 | **`cmpt-compact-sigma.pdf` Remark 6.1's stated justification is false, and a reader who trusts it builds a key-extraction bug.** The paper justifies its nonce construction with *"Including the full witness ensures nonces are unique per transaction."* **The witness is byte-identical across a resequenced or version-bumped resubmission of the same transfer**, while `TransactionContextID` — and therefore the challenge — is not. Deriving nonces over the paper's `statement` omits it, producing two proofs with shared commitments under distinct challenges. ⚠ **That reading of `statement` is not settled**: §6.2 enumerates the challenge hash's inputs with `TransactionContextID` as item 4 and closes *"Omitting any statement element from the hash would allow cross-protocol attacks"*, and upstream's prover names its variable `stmt_hash` and folds the context into it. The filing reports the ambiguity and its consequence, not the x reading alone (`REVIEW-048c` §A). The recommendation is sound; the reason given for it is not | **Found independently by two cryptography reviewers, 2026-08-17**, from the same primary sources with no sight of each other (REVIEW-005a §3.1, REVIEW-005b §0.1; adjudicated REVIEW-005c §1). ⚠ **Upstream's implementation is NOT affected on the utility path and is not relying on its salt**: `src/proof_compact_standard.c` hashes `context_id` into `stmt_hash` after the point block, as do the convertback, clawback and pok_sk provers — verified at `85dbac7`. **But the guard is `if (context_id)` and `proof_pok_sk.c` documents it as `/* context_id is optional */`, so a caller passing NULL reintroduces the full bug.** ⚠ Corrected 2026-10-18: it does not. `generate_deterministic_nonces` has mixed a fresh `RAND_bytes` salt into every call since #18 in April 2026, so a NULL context ID omits the context from the hash but does not repeat nonces (E-315). File as a paper correction plus a caller-error surface, not as an implementation defect. The caller-error half is the item Trail of Bits' Appendix B already raises (E-296), so the draft credits it there or drops it (E-313). Still holds at mpt-crypto `1.0.5` on reading (E-306). Ruled to file as an erratum (E-308). **Filed 2026-10-18 as [mpt-crypto#153](https://github.com/XRPLF/mpt-crypto/issues/153)** after three review rounds, as the ambiguity and its consequence, with the caller-error half dropped (E-315) | A |
| F-18 | **The published Rust FFI crate silently links an mpt-crypto four tag-generations behind rippled's, and it is not on the mainline branch.** `mpt-crypto-sys@0.1.0-beta.1` sets `MPT_CRYPTO_VERSION = "0.3.0-rc2"` and downloads a prebuilt-dylib release tarball under a SHA-256 pin. `0.3.0-rc2` (commit `83d9b13`) is **older than the `0.4.0-rc4` shipped in rippled 3.3.0**, and older than `1.0.2` and `1.0.4`. **Two distinct failure modes, and they are opposite in character.** (i) Left alone, `cargo build` **succeeds** and links `0.3.0-rc2` — the crate's vendored `secp256k1_mpt.h` is **byte-identical** to the header in the tarball it fetches, so nothing is inconsistent and nothing warns; you simply get an old prover. (ii) Redirected at a current library via `MPT_CRYPTO_LIB_DIR` (→ E-61), the drift becomes loud: the `0.3.0-rc2` header declares **seven functions that no longer exist at `1.0.4`** — `secp256k1_elgamal_pedersen_link_prove`/`_verify`, `secp256k1_equality_plaintext_prove`/`_verify`, `secp256k1_mpt_proof_equality_shared_r_size`, `secp256k1_mpt_prove_equality_shared_r`, `secp256k1_mpt_verify_equality_shared_r` — so a caller touching any of them gets undefined symbols. **Upstream removed exported functions between the pinned version and current**, which is the direction that matters: the stale side has *more* API, not less. Separately, both crates were published from `XRPLF/xrpl-rust` commit `d36ef38`, path `internal/mpt-crypto-sys`, and **`d36ef38` is the head of the unmerged branch `mpt-crypto-beta-release` only** — the path does not exist on `main` or at the `v1.2.0` release | Verified 2026-08-18 by downloading the crate and the `0.3.0-rc2` natives tarball (20 MB, prebuilt dylibs for six platforms; the fetch path **works**, contrary to a first reading here that it might 404), diffing headers, and resolving the publish commit's branch membership via the GitHub API. Crates.io re-queried the same day: still exactly one version each, 2026-06-08 (E-57). ⚠ **Failure mode (ii) is read, not executed** — no Rust binary was linked, so which of the seven a real signer would touch is undetermined. Mode (i) is established by the byte-identical header diff. Overtaken the same day: `0.1.0`, published 2026-08-18, pins `1.0.4`, and its path is on xrpl-rust `main`. Recorded not-to-file (E-298, E-299) | **B — read, no Rust build attempted** |
| F-24 | **Our own facility walkthrough understates what a ConvertBack publishes.** `FACILITY-WALKTHROUGH-2026-08-20.md` §3 step 9 says *"Public: the aggregate change"*, and §4's disclosure table is written the same way. **`MPTAmount` is a plaintext field on every individual `ConfidentialMPTConvertBack`** (XLS-0096 §10.3), so an observer sees **each withdrawal's own amount**, not merely the net movement in `sfConfidentialOutstandingAmount`. The two ConvertBacks of E-131 read 95 and 191 straight off the validated transactions. ⚠ **This is a finding against THIS PROJECT's document, not against the specification** — XLS-0096 §10.1 and its own §10.7 worked example are explicit that the ConvertBack amount is revealed. The walkthrough's sentence would let a reader conclude that retiring a facility in tranches hides the tranche sizes. It does not | **2026-08-25, executed.** Read back off validated ledgers 4743286 and 4743289. Corrected in the walkthrough the same day; recorded here because a wrong disclosure sentence is exactly what `CLAIMS.md` exists to catch, and this one survived the walkthrough's own `claims-check` | A, executed |
| F-25 | **`tapFailHard` cannot be bound into a transaction. It is a submission-time choice, and only the submitter can make it.** `TapFailHard` is an `ApplyFlags` value, not a transaction flag: it enters through the `submit` RPC's `fail_hard` parameter (rippled 3.3.0 `Submit.cpp:32-37` → `NetworkOPs.cpp:1683-1684`) and is not a field of the signed blob. ⚠ **This is why B-15's Part C is a convention rather than a property, and it is a structural reason rather than a procedural one.** A signer that wanted its transaction never to publish a `tec` **cannot express that wish** — there is nothing to sign that carries it. Whoever holds the blob decides, and a signer that does not submit has handed the decision away by construction. The decision request said *"nothing forces a separate submitter to set the flag"*; this is why nothing can | **Read at source 2026-08-25**, rippled `3.3.0`/`00a178f`. **Not a defect in rippled** — the flag is a node-local application policy and belongs where it is. It is a consequence for any design that separates signing from submission, which is what ADR-0001 accepted and what architecture §1 calls load-bearing in both halves | A (E-162) |
| F-26 | ⚠ **The facility walkthrough's *"After this, no plaintext amount appears again until the facility is retired or resized"* was FALSE, and it is the SECOND false disclosure sentence in that document.** Step 5 — supplier enrolment — publishes **two** plaintext amounts per supplier: the bank's `Payment` of the enrolment token, and the supplier's own `ConfidentialMPTConvert`, whose `MPTAmount` is a plaintext field exactly as step 4's and step 9's are. On the document's own worked example — thirty suppliers — that is **sixty plaintext amounts** between the float conversion and the first drawdown, all of them after the sentence says none appear. **What is true is narrower and is what the design actually buys**: no *drawdown* and no *repayment* publishes an amount, and those are the numbers that carry the commercial signal. ⚠ **Like F-24, it survived the document's own `claims-check`, and like F-24 it is about WHICH amounts are plaintext.** A sentence of the form *"no plaintext amount appears after X"* should be assumed wrong until every transaction type between X and the end has been listed | **Found 2026-08-25 by `REVIEW-022`'s Codex leg**, reading the walkthrough against `run-facility.ts`'s enrolment path — which repeated the same sentence, so the code and the explainer were wrong together. Corrected in both, and in §4's disclosure table, which named `Convert` and `ConvertBack` and omitted the plaintext `Payment`. ⚠ **Read at source and not executed**: ~~the facility has never run~~ **the facility had not yet run when this was written; it ran on 2026-08-25** (**B-18**, E-146), so this is a reading of XLS-0096 §7.2's field table against this design's own enrolment step | A on the reading, NOT executed (E-162) |
| F-27 | **`ConfidentialMPTConvert` deposits into the holder's INBOX, not into their spendable balance — a `ConfidentialMPTMergeInbox` is required before any of it can be sent.** Read off the ledger on 2026-08-25 after a validated Convert of 5000 on `rQfHjoAYnpSSuvqoYK1eYTiBjoT3az65pB`: `ConfidentialBalanceSpending` decrypted to **0** and `ConfidentialBalanceInbox` to **5000**. ⚠ **This project had the fact in front of it three times and never wrote it down** — every earlier driver (`spike-2026-08-17/convert.ts`, `spike-step3`, `spike-step6`) performs a MergeInbox immediately after its Convert, and one of them even labels it *"not a signer operation"* — so the sequence was copied without the reason ever being recorded. The facility's balance book therefore modelled a Convert as crediting `spending`, and the FIRST Devnet run refused to reconcile: expected 5000, ledger said 0 | **2026-08-25, executed.** ⚠ **The then-existing tests could not have caught it, and none did**: the suite asserts this project's model of the protocol against itself, and only the ledger says what the protocol does. ⚠ **A unit test absolutely CAN encode the rule once the ledger has established it** — `facility/tests/book.test.ts` does, and fails if `convert` credits `spending` again. This row said *"no unit test could have caught it"*; `REVIEW-023` C-7 ruled that a fabricated absolute inside a correction, the fix reached `book.ts` and **not this row**, and round two found it still standing. `CLAUDE.md`'s standard arriving from the other side — the transaction returned `tesSUCCESS` and **the model of what it did was wrong**. Fixed in `facility/src/book.ts` (`convert` credits the inbox) and `facility/src/facility.ts` (`convertFloat` and `enrolSupplier` merge afterwards), with the ledger reading quoted at both sites so the next reader does not have to re-derive it | A, executed |
| F-28 | ⚠ **`tapFailHard` stops a `tec` reaching the LEDGER. It does not stop the ciphertext reaching the NODE you submitted to — and under a remote ledger source that node is the one F-23 is about.** The **signer** reads the destination's `HolderEncryptionKey` and the issuance's `IssuerEncryptionKey` from an endpoint, builds a `ConfidentialMPTSend` encrypted to whatever it read, proves and signs it; the **facility** then **submits that blob to the same endpoint**, because `run-facility.ts` hands the signer its own `ledgerEndpoint`. ⚠ **Both halves of that sentence were wrong for one commit** — it said *"the facility reads"*, which is the wrong component, and *"the issuer's `HolderEncryptionKey`"*, which is a field the issuer does not have. Round two's two legs found one each. ⚠ **The endpoints being the same is a property of this driver, not of `Facility`'s constructor.** A lying source therefore receives the ciphertext encrypted under its own key **before** the flag can prevent application or relay. The flag prevents *publication*; it prevents no *disclosure to the submitter*. ⚠ **So under E-151's amendment the two halves of B-15's answer are not merely unequal in strength — Part C does not cover the residual Part A was carrying at all.** With loopback the node is the operator's own, **which narrows the question to whoever can reach that node — and the issue #1 ruling puts a hostile same-host process IN SCOPE.** ⚠ **This row said the question *does not arise* on loopback, and round two's Codex leg pointed at `facility/src/loopback.ts`'s own header to refute it**: that module says in terms that the same-host adversary *"can point this at a different endpoint, or edit the local node's answers"*. A compromised local node lies about the key and reads the submitted blob exactly as a remote one does. What is true is narrower: **against an honest, operator-controlled node the disclosure has nowhere to go**, and that is the reason the ruling called loopback the protection and the flag a seatbelt | **Read at source and reasoned, 2026-08-25; NOT executed.** Found by `REVIEW-023`'s Codex leg while checking whether the executed control established what four documents said it did. ⚠ **What was executed is narrower than those documents claimed**: `tapFailHard` changed the treatment of a `tecNO_AUTH` from a plaintext `Payment` — no substituted key, no `ConfidentialMPTSend`, no `tecBAD_PROOF`, no attacker-key ciphertext. The "all `tec`" step is `Transactor.cpp:1645-1651`'s text, not this run's. **What would settle it:** a substituted-key Send under the flag against a lying source that **does** receive the submission — and then showing whether that source can recover the amount despite `fail_hard`. ⚠ **This row first prescribed *"a source that cannot obtain the blob"*, which is a different topology and cannot exhibit the disclosure F-28 is about.** Round two's Codex leg found it: an experiment that removes the mechanism cannot promote the row from reasoned to executed. The wording was inherited from `REVIEW-023c` §5, which said the same thing | A on the reading, NOT executed (E-162) |
| F-23 | **A wrong ledger read is a CONFIDENTIALITY break, not a rejected transaction — and the signer cannot control which.** A `ConfidentialMPTSend` encrypts the amount to the destination's `HolderEncryptionKey` and the issuer's `IssuerEncryptionKey`, both read from the ledger. rippled verifies against the keys **it** holds — `ConfidentialMPTSend.cpp:132` takes `(*sleDestinationMPToken)[sfHolderEncryptionKey]` — so a substituted key fails the proof. It fails with **`tecBAD_PROOF`** (`ConfidentialTransfer.cpp:451`; `TER.h:371` makes it 199, a `tec`), and `Transactor.cpp:1653-1656` with `isTecClaimHardFail` (`applySteps.h:40`) **applies a `tec` to the ledger** unless the submitter set `tapFailHard`. So an adversary who controls what the signer reads gets the holder to publish the amount encrypted under a key the adversary holds, **and the failed transaction keeps it on chain**. The signer does not submit, so it does not get to set that flag | ~~**Read at source 2026-08-19.** ⚠ **Not executed**~~ — **EXECUTED 2026-08-19 and it HELD.** `tools/spike-step2-2026-08-19/f23-substituted-key.ts` built a Send encrypting the amount to an adversary's key instead of the destination's registered one. `tecBAD_PROOF`, **recorded on validated ledger 4603815**, hash `7A283EE5920EBB97AB90F4BC92B4E4E293C6DCE541A9607277D45DA4145AF160`. The amount **42 was recovered from public ledger data by the adversary**, holding only the key they supplied; **the intended destination could not decrypt it at all**. The published ciphertext is byte-identical to what was submitted. ⚠ **This is what a wrong READ costs. It is not a network attack** — nothing was compromised. Mitigated, not closed: TLS is required for any non-loopback endpoint, the signer re-reads the destination key before signing, and it checks the ledger's `HolderEncryptionKey` for **its own** account against the key it holds — **there is no such anchor for the destination's key or the issuer's** (B-15) | A, executed |
| F-22 | **This project's own signer built a four-participant proof and serialised three ciphertexts, and no test or Devnet run could see it.** `prover::prepare_send` encrypts to the auditor when the issuance carries an `AuditorEncryptionKey`, and includes that participant in the proof; `WireFields::Send` had no `AuditorEncryptedAmount` and `txn::builder` emitted none. Every `ConfidentialMPTSend` on an auditor-enabled issuance would have carried a proof over four participants against three on-chain ciphertexts. ⚠ **This is OUR defect, not upstream's, and is not fileable.** It is recorded here because of *how it hid*: the Devnet issuance used for every execution in this project has **no auditor key**, and no test fixture had one either, so a whole configuration branch of the protocol had zero coverage while looking fully exercised. **Both `REVIEW-015` legs found it independently** — the only finding in the round that both found | **Found 2026-08-18**, fixed with `fixture_with_auditor` and a test that decrypts the auditor's ciphertext under the auditor's own key. ⚠ **The correct field code is `nth=43`; a leg proposed 42, which is `AuditorEncryptedBalance`** — a different field on a different ledger object. Taking the suggested code would have produced a transaction that encodes cleanly and means something else. Checked against `ripple-binary-codec`'s definitions | A (E-162) |
| F-21 | **Devnet rejects a transaction that carries an explicit `NetworkID`, and ~~nothing a signer author reads says which networks those are~~ the codec's `definitions.json` does not say which networks those are, although xrpl.org does (E-307).** A `ConfidentialMPTSend` including `NetworkID: 2` was rejected with `telNETWORK_ID_MAKES_TX_NON_CANONICAL` — *"Transactions submitted to this node/network must NOT include a NetworkID field."* Networks **at or below 1024** must omit it; above, they require it. The threshold is not in the field's `definitions.json` entry, which is where a signer author building from the codec looks, and there is no hint that a correctly-typed, correctly-encoded field is conditionally forbidden. ⚠ **This is a usability finding about where the rule lives, not a defect in rippled** — the rule itself is deliberate and the error message is clear once you hit it. The consequence for this project is real and visible: the confirmation cannot render a network number out of a Devnet transaction, because there is none, and it says so | **Observed 2026-08-18** on Devnet, rippled 3.3.0, submitting a blob the signer produced. Fixed in `signer/src/txn.rs`, which omits the field at or below `xrpl::NETWORK_ID_OMIT_AT_OR_BELOW`. → `UPSTREAM-FEEDBACK.md` U-39. **Not filed**. Re-run on 2026-09-14, and Devnet still refuses the field (E-307). Ruled not-to-file (E-308) | A, executed (E-162) |
| F-19 | **mpt-crypto's exported PoK API makes account binding optional, and the public header does not say so.** `secp256k1_mpt_pok_sk_verify(ctx, proof, pk, context_id)` accepts a NULL `context_id` — `/* context_id is optional */` in `proof_pok_sk.c`, and `build_pok_challenge` folds the context in only `if (context_id)`. With NULL, the challenge is `SHA-256("CMPT_POK_SK_REGISTER" ‖ pk ‖ T)`: a valid registration proof bound to **nothing but the public key**, and therefore **replayable by any party who has seen it, on any account, on any chain**. The **public header** `include/secp256k1_mpt.h:219–224` declares both `_prove` and `_verify` with the parameter and ~~**no doc comment whatsoever**~~ no doc comment that mentions `context_id` (E-296) — the optionality and its cost are visible only by reading the `.c`. ⚠ **This does NOT affect the ledger.** rippled cannot reach the NULL path: the utility wrapper `mpt_verify_convert_proof` rejects a NULL context hash before forwarding (E-66), and rippled always supplies a `uint256`. **Never describe this as an XLS-96 vulnerability.** The exposure is to *other* consumers of the exported library — a Rust or WASM binding, or this project's own signer harness — for whom passing NULL compiles, runs, verifies, and silently produces a forgeable registration | **Read at source 2026-08-18** at `85dbac7` = `1.0.4`: `proof_pok_sk.c` (`build_pok_challenge`, both `MPT_ARG_CHECK` blocks), `include/secp256k1_mpt.h:211–224`, `src/utility/mpt_utility.cpp:864`. Found while closing issue #4. → `UPSTREAM-FEEDBACK.md` U-38. **Not filed** — nothing is filed without first checking the April Trail of Bits report and its fix-review appendix. Checked 2026-09-14: the report raises this in Appendices B and D, its fix review does not track either, and upstream has documented the field as optional on one function but not made it required (E-296). Ruled not-to-file by the owner (E-298) | A (E-162) |
| F-20 | **`REVIEW-012-RULING-keygen`'s domain-separation table named a label family that does not exist.** Its row for `r`/`ρ` read *"the `WCONF-R-*` / `WCONF-RHO-*` family"*. REVIEW-010's Deliverable A defines **one** label, `WCONF-RANDOM-SCALAR-v1`, with two role strings `elgamal-r` and `balance-rho`. ⚠ **Domain separation was never at risk** — `WCONF-ELGAMAL-KEYGEN-v1` is disjoint from `WCONF-RANDOM-SCALAR-v1` either way — but a table whose entire purpose is to demonstrate disjointness must name what it is comparing against. **This is an internal defect, not an upstream one, and is not fileable.** It is recorded here because it is the exact failure mode this register exists for: a document that was correct in its conclusion and wrong in its evidence, written by the session that also adjudicated it | **Found independently by both REVIEW-013 legs**, 2026-08-18. Corrected in place in `REVIEW-012-RULING-keygen-2026-08-18.md` §3 with the correction noted rather than silently applied | A |
| F-16 | **A `LoanSet` in which both parties signed the same pre-`CounterpartySignature` payload was accepted, first attempt.** The borrower signs as `Account`, the lender as `Counterparty`, both over `encodeForSigning(tx)` computed before the signature object is inserted. ⚠ **Narrowed 2026-08-17 after both REVIEW-007 legs called the original wording overstated**, and they were right on two counts. The script never asserts that `encodeForSigning` is *unchanged* after inserting `CounterpartySignature` — it reuses one pre-computed variable — so it demonstrates the construction is accepted, not that `isSigningField: false` is the mechanism. And the counterparty signs a payload carrying the **borrower's** `SigningPubKey` in the top-level header, which an integrator would not guess; "no field-ordering trick" was wrong | Executed 2026-08-17 → `tesSUCCESS`, loan `3B9A398E531D4CF2CD85C9867FA57371E1696DCE536E7FF643D4B74469904050`. `ripple-binary-codec@2.9.0` marks `CounterpartySignature` `isSigningField: false` correctly, which is what makes the payload identical for both signers. Overtaken: re-run on 2026-09-14, Devnet refuses this construction under `fixCleanup3_4_0` (E-305). Ruled not-to-file (E-308) | A |
| F-12 | **mpt-crypto's shipped WASM build script fails at emsdk 6.0.6 and succeeds at its CI-pinned 6.0.2.** `.github/scripts/build-wasm.sh` links the final module with `emcc`, but `src/utility/mpt_utility.cpp` is C++, so the link needs libc++. At **6.0.6 it fails** with `undefined symbol: operator new(unsigned long)`, `__cxa_throw`, `vtable for std::length_error` and four more. Emscripten's own warning names the fix — *"Try linking with 'em++' or passing '-sDEFAULT_TO_CXX'"* — and adding `-sDEFAULT_TO_CXX` to the two `emcc` link lines is sufficient at `85dbac7` (at `1.0.5` there are three, `build-wasm.sh:363`, `:365` and `:374`, the third linking the browser glue; E-299): nothing else in the script needed changing. ⚠ **Narrowed 2026-08-17, self-caught before review. The first wording said "fails on any emsdk newer than its CI pin"** — an extrapolation from a single newer version. **Two versions were tested, not a range**; where between 6.0.2 and 6.0.6 the behaviour changes is unknown, and so is anything above 6.0.6. ⚠ Both are now known: 6.0.3, 6.0.4 and 6.0.5 build, and 6.0.6, 6.0.10 and 6.0.12 fail, because Emscripten 6.0.6 disabled `DEFAULT_TO_CXX` by default, and all three link lines need `em++` (E-315) | Executed 2026-08-17 at `85dbac7`, macOS/arm64. Both versions built from the same source tree; 6.0.2 produced `mpt_crypto.wasm` (2 110 544 B), 6.0.6 failed at step 5 and produced nothing. **The pin is doing real work and is not documented as load-bearing** — the comment beside `version: 6.0.2` says only "pinned for reproducible wasm output … the emsdk version does not change crypto results", which is true and understates it. Anyone following the script's own header ("Prerequisites: Emscripten SDK on PATH") without reading the CI workflow gets a link failure. Still holds at `1.0.5` on reading, not re-run (E-299). Re-run 2026-09-28 and 2026-10-18 at `3baaaac`, with the second run's logs kept under `docs/evidence/f12-emsdk-2026-10-18/`. Ruled to file (E-302). **Filed 2026-10-18 as [mpt-crypto#154](https://github.com/XRPLF/mpt-crypto/issues/154)** after two review rounds, the first reproducing the builds independently (E-315) | A |
| F-13 | **`cmpt-compact-sigma.pdf` §2.1's "transmitted once on-chain" is not true of rippled 3.3.0.** The paper's Table 2 totals a Send at 1177 B for n=4 on the basis that all ciphertexts share `C₁` and it is sent once. `C₁` **is** shared, and sharing is **protocol-enforced, not a property of our client**: a Send carrying independent per-participant randomness is refused by the prover, by `mpt_verify_send_proof`, and by rippled with `tecBAD_PROOF`, against a shared-`C₁` control returning `tesSUCCESS` (REVIEW-004c §3). But each field is a full 66-byte pair on the wire, so `C₁` is transmitted **once per participant**. Real size is **1210 B without an auditor and 1276 B with one, both measured**; the 99 B gap is exactly the three redundant `C₁` copies at n=4 | Executed 2026-08-17 → E-4, E-45. Two readings are open and this session cannot separate them: either the paper describes an intended optimisation the implementation has not taken, or it describes the protocol loosely and the wire format was never meant to match. **Settle which before filing** — as a size-optimisation suggestion it is worth ~99 B/tx; as an erratum it is a one-line correction. Check whether an `sfSharedC1`-style layout was considered. ⚠ Not settled, and the filing asks upstream which: correct the totals, or mark the layout a proposal the protocol did not adopt (E-315). Still holds at both upstream HEADs on reading, and the sizes were not re-measured (E-304). Ruled to file as an erratum (E-308). **Filed 2026-10-18 as [mpt-crypto#155](https://github.com/XRPLF/mpt-crypto/issues/155)**, led by XLS-0096 §17.1, which computes 1276 and 1210 bytes outright (E-315) | A |
| F-15 | **The published JS wrapper ~~makes the tight-window decryption optimisation unreachable~~ keeps the tight-window decryption optimisation out of its hex API, reachable only through raw WASM calls (E-301).** The C API is `mpt_decrypt_amount(ct, priv, out, range_low, range_high)`; `@xrplf/mpt-crypto@0.1.0` exposes `decryptAmount(ciphertext, privateKey, rangeHigh)` and **fixes `range_low` to 0 internally**. So JS cost is always O(rangeHigh) from zero, and F-7's central result — that a 10¹⁶ balance decrypts in ~5 ms given a narrow window, because cost tracks window *width* not value magnitude — ~~cannot be obtained from the published API at all~~ cannot be obtained from `decryptAmount`, and can be from the exported `loadWasmModule` (E-301). An integrator tracking expected balances off-ledger (which F-7/U-21 say they must) has nowhere to put that knowledge | Executed 2026-08-17 against `@xrplf/mpt-crypto@0.1.0`, decrypting a live Devnet ciphertext whose true value is 150. Indicative timings, Apple M3 Pro, **n=1 per point, not a benchmark under P-8**: `rangeHigh` 1 000 → 42.6 ms; 100 000 → 485.6 ms; 1 000 000 → 4 259.6 ms — linear, as the fixed floor implies. The same ciphertext with a native `[149,151]` window returns in 21.6 ms *including process spawn*. **The structural claim, not the timings, is the finding**: `range_low` is absent from the wrapper's signature. Verified by reading `dist/esm/primitives.js:66–79` and `dist/primitives.d.ts:33`, so it is not an inference from timing. `index.d.ts`, which exports `loadWasmModule`, was not cited, and a run through it on 2026-09-14 reached the narrow window (E-301). Ruled not-to-file (E-302) | A on the API shape; timings indicative only |
| F-14 | **`tecINSUFFICIENT_FUNDS` misattributes the cause when a holder's balance is partly confidential — on vault AND lending transactors.** A holder owning 150 confidential units of an issuance, with 50 public units left, was refused a 100-unit `VaultDeposit` with *"Not enough funds available to complete requested transaction."* The holder **does** own the units; they are in a form the vault cannot see. The error names the wrong thing, and an integrator has no signal pointing at confidentiality | **Reproduced on a second, independent transactor 2026-08-17**: a borrower holding 5 public and 600 confidential units was refused a 120-unit `LoanPay` with the identical code and message. So this is not a quirk of one vault handler — it is what the whole stack does when value is held confidentially. Executed → E-50, F-10. **This is the concrete, integrator-visible face of F-10** and it is stronger evidence than F-10's current form, because it is a wrong answer rather than a missing warning. Same filing constraint applies: settle F-10's competing reading first (an issuance may legitimately be confidential-capable while a vault operates on its public balance), since if that reading holds this is an error-message request rather than a composability defect | A, executed (E-162) |
| F-29 | **XLS-0096 §7.4 states the `MPToken` deletion blocker unconditionally; rippled enforces it through two paths that disagree.** §7.4: a holder's `MPToken` *"cannot be deleted from the ledger once confidential fields have been initialized, even if"* all three balances hold the canonical encrypted zero. `MPTokenHelpers.cpp:300–307` (`removeEmptyHolding`) matches it — unconditional, on the presence of any of the **four** confidential fields. `MPTokenAuthorize.cpp:86–103` (`tfMPTUnauthorize`, the holder deleting their own holding) does **not**: it returns `tecHAS_OBLIGATIONS` only when the **issuance-level** `sfConfidentialOutstandingAmount != 0`, and then tests only **two** of the four fields. With issuance-wide confidential outstanding at 0, the holding is deletable with confidential fields present. ⚠ **Not a supply-accounting defect** — a zero issuance-wide total means every holder's confidential balance is provably zero, and the inline comment says that is the reasoning. **The consequence is for integrators: the reserve is NOT permanently locked, which is what §7.4's wording would have an onboarding flow tell a user.** ⚠ **P-10 is NOT weakened and must not be read as weakened**: XLS-96 still has no key rotation and no key recovery. Deleting and recreating a holding is not rotation, and the upstream filing says so explicitly before raising the question of whether the sequence is intended ⚠ **CORRECTED 2026-08-26, same day, by a second review pass — and the correction was posted to the issue.** The conditional behaviour is **deliberate and pinned by upstream tests**: `ConfidentialTransfer_test.cpp:3156` expects `tecHAS_OBLIGATIONS` *"even if he has encrypted zero amount"* while confidential outstanding is non-zero, and `:3164` / `:3249` expect the unauthorize to **succeed** — *"can delete mptoken if outstanding confidential balance is zero"*. The unconditional path is tested too, for vault shares (`Vault_test.cpp:5972`, `ConfidentialTransfer_test.cpp:3256`). **So the two paths do not disagree by oversight; they encode different policies and both are intended, which means the defect is §7.4's WORDING and nothing else.** The filing had ended by asking a maintainer which behaviour was intended — a question the test suite answers, and which should have been checked before filing rather than after. | **2026-08-26, source reading at rippled 3.3.0. ⚠ NOT EXECUTED** — the deletion sequence has not been run on Devnet, and the filing says so in its own disclosure. Filed upstream as [XRPL-Standards #610](https://github.com/XRPLF/XRPL-Standards/issues/610) / `UPSTREAM-FEEDBACK.md` U-50. ⚠ **Found by re-verifying U-11 against the transactor rather than against the spec** — U-11 had been graded A on a reading of §7.4 alone for ten days. **Resolved upstream:** XLS-0096 PR #604, merged 2026-09-03, moved the rule to §7.2 and states both policies, matching the code; #610 was closed on 2026-09-10 (E-316). §7.2 read at `58ccd30` on 2026-10-18 | A, not executed |

**Headline for publication:** with F-3 and F-4 retired, the surviving documentation finding is **F-1**, which is now on the canonical xrpl.org page rather than a prerelease one, plus errata F-5 and F-6. **F-7 and F-8 are the strongest new material and neither is in the Trail of Bits report** (one of two audits, B-30) — F-8 in particular is a dangling API reference that any integrator will hit the moment they need to decrypt above the default window.

**Revised headline, 2026-08-16:** **F-10 now leads.** Two amendments enabled on the same network that silently do not compose is a larger finding than any documentation erratum here, it has no existing coverage, and the Trail of Bits report does not touch it — its scope was XLS-96, not XLS-96 against XLS-66. It is also the one finding here that is *not* yet fileable: it rests on 2 of 15 transactor paths executed, and GAP-8 must close first.

**Third revision, 2026-08-17.** F-10's headline position is unchanged, but **F-14 is now the better artifact for it** — a holder refused a deposit of units they own, with an error that names the wrong cause, is a reproducible wrong answer rather than an absent warning. Both still wait on GAP-8 and on F-10's unresolved competing reading. Separately, **F-12 is the only item here that is cheap, uncontested and fileable today**: a one-flag build fix with a reproduction on two emsdk versions, no dependency on any other open question. If exactly one thing goes upstream from this session, it is F-12.

**Nothing here is filed without first checking the Trail of Bits report and its fix-review appendix** (standing rule, `ADDENDUM-2026-08-16.md` §2). That includes Appendices B to D, which the fix review does not cover and where F-19 already was (E-296). F-7 is *specifically* a correction to how TOB-RIPCTX-1 has been read in this repository — it must not be filed as a novel finding. Since B-30, the check covers two audits, and the Sherlock one cannot be read. **File first, publish second.**

## Retracted

| # | Was claimed | Corrected to | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-1 | TASK ZERO §9 rev 1: the restructured facility "shows nothing about which supplier drew how much" | Amounts are hidden; counterparty graph, event timing and drawdown frequency are public | Found independently by all three review passes. Sender and receiver are public on every transfer |
| R-2 | ADR-0001 rev 1: local proving is "the only option under which the confidentiality claim is true" | Local proving preserves spending authority; issuer visibility is unchanged | Conflated custody with confidentiality — the exact distinction the ADR exists to draw |
| R-3 | F-3: "docs say confidentiality can be toggled on and off" | Withdrawn 2026-08-16 | Corrected upstream. The canonical xrpl.org page states enabling is one-way |
| R-4 | F-4: "docs say clawback resets the version counter" | Withdrawn 2026-08-16 | Not a doc error. TOB-RIPCTX-11, a Medium implementation bug found by Trail of Bits in April 2026 and since Resolved. The prerelease docs described pre-fix behaviour |
| R-6 | B-11 / ADR-0002 blocker 3 / investigation §12: that encrypted loan accounting is **structurally** blocked, because XLS-96's additive homomorphism "cannot support amortisation, which multiplies a balance by a rate" | Withdrawn 2026-08-17. The residual true statement is B-11 as rewritten | **The mathematics was wrong.** Scalar multiplication by a *public* scalar is native to an additively homomorphic scheme: for `(C₁,C₂) = (rG, mG+rP)`, scaling both components by public `k` yields `(krG, kmG+krP)`, which decrypts to `k·m`. An interest rate is a public loan parameter. What is genuinely non-linear is rounding and fixed-point truncation — and the remedy for that is a per-payment ZK proof, which is the pattern XLS-96 already uses. **Found independently by both REVIEW-003 legs**, and verified algebraically before acceptance. It was graded C and its inference was stated, which is what made it checkable — but a stated inference that is wrong is still wrong |
| R-5 | TASK ZERO §9: the vault converts the whole facility float in one transaction | ~~Retracted 2026-08-16 per ADDENDUM §1~~ — **un-retracted 2026-08-16 (same day), see F-7** | The retraction was correct about the mechanism and wrong about the consequence. A 10,000,000-unit balance is **not** undecryptable: decryption takes a caller-supplied window, and the vault — which tracks its own float off-ledger anyway — decrypts it in ~5 ms with a narrow window. §9's single-conversion design **stands**. What must be added is not a float ceiling but a **balance-tracking requirement** and a resynchronisation cost budget |
